PUBLIC NOTICE: 
This meeting of the Board is open to the public and will be held via Zoom conferencing.  If members of 
the community would like to participate, the meeting can be accessed via computer or mobile browser 
at: https://zoom.us/j/2489521600 Meeting ID: 248 952 1600 or by phone at: 253-215-8782 or 301-715-
8592 Meeting ID: 248 952 1600 .  Please note that all meeting participants will be initially placed in a 
“silent/virtual waiting room” until the meeting begins, at which point audio and video will be activated.  
It is recommended that you connect via computer audio, if poss ible.  If you choose to dial into the 
meeting from a phone, you may encounter a busy signal if Zoom systems are overloaded.  If that occurs, 
please either redial or try the alternative number provided.  We appreciate your patience as we navigate 
this meeting remotely.   
To submit a request to address the Troy School District Board of Education during  the Public 
comment portion of  this Virtual Board Meeting, please click on the following link  to complete a 
request form : https://tinyurl.com/ul9hprt.  Guidelines for Public Participation at Board Meetings can 
be viewed at the this link: https://tinyurl.com/vpl6fn5. 
The subjects to be discussed or considered or upon which any formal action may be taken are as listed 
below. Items do not have to be taken in the order shown on this meeting notice.  Unless removed from 
the consent agenda, items identified within the consent agenda will be acted on at one time.

* All meetings, except closed sessions, are open to the public.  The Board welcomes citizens’ comments.  The public participation 
portion of the meeting is limited to one -half hour for discussion of agenda items and one -half hour for non -agenda items.  Each 
person will be allowed to speak for up to three minutes, except where the number of speakers  exceeds the time limit.  In those 
instances, the President may reduce the time limit for each speaker in order to accommodate all of the individuals who wish t o 
address the Board of Education.  In any event, no one’s right to address the Board will be denied.

2. RECOGNITION 
A.  2019-20 Outstanding Support Staff Award    
The Troy School District has many outstanding support staff employees. This evening we 
are proud to recognize the accomplishments of 14 individuals who were nominated for the 
Troy School District Outstanding Support Staff Award. 
The nominees were chosen for this distinction by their colleagues, by parents and by former 
and current students, because they make a difference for our students each and every day. 
They exemplify our values and help us to ensure that when we say we ensure learning for 
all - ALL truly means ALL. 
We are grateful for their dedication and passion for our students. 
Sririsha Bathala, Professional Support to Educators, Hamilton Elementary School 
Thriveni Boreddy, Professional Support to Educators, Schroeder Elementary School 
George Freeman, Technology, Morse and Hill Elementary Schools 
MaryJo Gale, Office Personnel, Niles Center 
Terry Henderson, Professional Support to Educators, Leonard Elementary School 
Sajia Julie, Professional Support to Educators, Hamilton Elementary School 
Mary Kate Kendro, Professional Support to Educators, Athens High School 
Gina Kitchen, Professional Support to Educators, Leonard Elementary School 
Barb McCoy, Professional Support to Educators, Hamilton Elementary School 
Jeanne Miller, Office Personnel, Athens High School 
Laurie Mitchener, Safety and Security, Wass Elementary School 
Tara Stonecipher, Professional Support to Educators, Leonard Elementary School 
Lynn Yagley, Professional Support to Educators, Leonard Elementary School 
AND 
Barb Pangle, Secretary, Hill Elementary School 
                               (Overall TSD Outstanding Support Staff) 
From this outstanding pool of nominees, Barb Pangle was awarded the Troy School District 
Outstanding Support Staff Employee of the Year. Congratulations, Barb! 
At this time, I would like to invite Barb Pangle to say a few words.

                 2. C.     05.19.20 
2.       RECOGNITION 
 C.  National Teacher Appreciation Week 
Teacher Appreciation Week is observed nationally each year in May. This year, 
Teacher Appreciation Week was celebrated May 4th – May 8th.  At tonight’s 
meeting, the Troy School District and Board of Education would like to 
recognize all of our outstanding teachers by adopting the following resolution: 
WHEREAS, throughout Troy and across the country, teachers inspire, 
nurture and open children’s minds to new ideas,  
WHEREAS, teachers promote and strengthen the principles of democratic 
equality, develop leaders and encourage critical thinking; and 
WHEREAS, Troy teachers set the bar for excellence in education and 
continually exceed the highest standards in the profession; and 
WHEREAS, the goal of Teacher Appreciation Week is to raise national and 
local awareness of teaching excellence in building a better society; now,  
THEREFORE BE IT  RESOLVED, that the Troy School District supports 
National Teacher Appreciation Week, May 4-8, 20 20, and encourages our 
schools and community to appropriately recognize the vital and changing role 
of teachers in imagining and creating a better future for society. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Read and adopt resolution. 
  Moved by:     
  Supported by:     
Roll call vote.  
Anne        
Gottlieb         
Hammond

p.m.

RECOGNITION  
Troy School District’s Outstanding 
Teachers   The  Troy  School  District  has  many  outstanding  teachers 
on  its  staff.  This  evening,  the  Board  recognized  the 
accomplishments of 44 individuals who were nominated 
for  the  Troy  School  District  Outstanding Teacher  Award 
Program.  The nominees represent exemplary educators 
at the elementary, middle and high school levels. They 
were  nominated  by  their  peers,  parents  and  by 
students.    These  teachers  have  had  a  tremendous 
impact on the lives of so man y through their dedication 
and commitment to their students.  
This  evening’s  celebration  is  a  little  different  than  we 
would  have  had  in  person,  but  these  incredible 
educators are no less deserving of this recognition.

Section 1 Property Sale 
Hauff that  
 20‐042 
WHEREAS,  the  School  District  issued  a  Request  For 
Proposals  to  obtain  offers  t o  purchase  certain  surplus 
real  property  situated  in  the  City  of  Troy,  Oakland 
County, Michigan, described as follows: 
6.12  +/‐  acres  of  vacant  land  located  on  the 
North  side  of  Square  Lake  Road,  East  of  John  R 
Road,  in  Troy,  Michigan  (the  “Premises”).  The 
Premises is part of a larg er 70.647 +/‐ acre parcel 
(Parcel 2). Additionally, the District is including a 
11.281  +/‐  acre  parcel  (Parcel  1).  The 
undeveloped portion of Par cel 2 and all of Parcel 
1 shall remain preserved in perpetuity through a 
conservation easement. 
WHEREAS,  the  School  District  has  received  an  Offer  To 
Purchase  Real  Estate  (the  “Offer”)  from  Robertson 
Board of Education Meeting  4  April 21, 2020 
35
It 
was  moved  by  Dr.  Philippart  and  supported  by  Mr. 
Brothers  Homes,  (the  “Purchaser”),  to  purchase  the 
Premises  for  a  minimum  purchase  price  of  One  Million 
Four  Hundred  Twenty  Five  Thousand  and  00/100 
($1,425,000.00)  Dollars  (the  “Purchase  Price”  with  an 
earnest  money  deposit  of  Fifty  Thousand  and  00/100 
($50,000.00) Dollars), and 
WHEREAS, the School District has reviewed the Offer and 
desires  to  authorize  the  Superintendent  of  the  School 
District, or his designee, to negotiate the other terms and 
c o n d i t i o n s  o f  t h e  O f f e r  t h a t  a r e  a c c e p t a b l e  t o  t h e  
Superintendent  after  consultation  with  legal  counsel,  on 
terms substantially similar to those described in the Offer 
and in the Request For Proposals. 
WHEREAS, once the Superintendent is satisfied with the 
negotiated Offer, the Superintendent, or his designee, is 
hereby authorized to execute the Offer as well as any and 
all  documents  necessary  and  incidental  to  the  purchase 
of the Premises on behalf of the School District. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
1. The  School  District  authorizes  the  sale  of  the
P r e m i s e s  f o r  a  m i n i m u m  p u r c h a s e  p r i c e  o f  O n e
Million Four Hundred Twenty Five Thousand and
00/100  ($1,425,000.00)  Dollars  and  further
authorizes  the  Superintendent  of  the  School
District,  or  his  designee,  to  negotiate  the  other
terms  and  conditions  of  the  Offer  that  are
acceptable  to  the  Superintendent  after
consultation  with  legal  counsel,  on  terms
substantially  similar  to  those  described  in  the
Offer and in the Request For Proposals.
2. Once  the  Superintendent  is  satisfied  with  the
negotiated  Offer,  the  Superintendent,  or  his
designee,  is  hereby  authorized  to  execute  the
Offer as well as any and all documents necessary
and  incidental  to  the  purchase  of  the  Premises
on behalf of the School District.

5. CONSENT 
 F. CONSIDERATION:  Approve Oakland Intermediate School 
District 2020-2021 Budget 
  In accordance with Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as amended, 
the Troy Board of Education is required to review the proposed Oakland 
Intermediate School District budget and express either its support or 
disapproval of this budget.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
  Moved by  
  Supported by  
WHEREAS: 
 1. Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as amended, requires the intermediate 
school board to submit its proposed budget no later than May 1 of each year to 
the board of each constituent district for review; and 
 2. Not later than June 1 of each year, the board of each constituent district shall 
review the proposed intermediate school district budget, shall adopt a board 
resolution expressing its support for or disapproval of the proposed intermediate 
school district budget, and shall submit to the intermediate school board any 
specific objections and proposed changes the constituent district board has to 
the budget. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 1. The Troy Board of Education has received and reviewed the proposed 
intermediate school district budget in accordance with Section 624 of the 
Revised School Code, as amended, and, by the adoption of this resolution, 
expresses its support for the proposed intermediate school district budget.  The 
Troy School District Board of Education believes that it is the responsibility of 
118
the Intermediate School District to be responsible for its own operations, 
including adoption of its budget.  The Troy School District’s s upport of the 
Oakland Intermediate School District’s budget is for the sole purpose of 
complying with Section 624 of the Revised School Code. 
 2. The secretary of the Troy Board of Education or his/her designee shall forward 
a copy of this resolution to the intermediate school board or its superintendent 
no later than June 1, 2020. 
 3. All resolutions insofar as they conflict with this resolution be and the same are 
hereby rescinded. 
Yes:  
No:  
Resolution declared adopted. 
 Secretary, Troy Board of Education 
The undersigned duly qualified and acting Secretary of the Board of Education of the 
Troy School District, Michigan, hereby certifies that the foregoing is a true and 
complete copy of a resolution adopted by the Troy Board of Education at a regular 
meeting held on May 19, 2020, the original of which resolution is part of the Board’s 
minutes, and further certifies that notice of the meeting was given to the public under 
the Open Meetings Act, 1976 PA 267, as amended. 
 Secretary, Troy Board of Education

120
March 24, 2020 
Dear Board of Education Designate: 
The Oakland Schools Board of Education and I invite you to review the enclosed proposed Oakland 
Schools budget for FY 2020-21.  As you know, Oakland Schools is primarily funded by  property tax 
dollars, and we are projecting a growth factor in Oakland Schools’ property tax revenues of 3.25%.  Tax 
revenue projections are conservative compared to Oakland County taxable value increase projections as 
there is concern that a Headlee rollback of our operating millage is possible for FY 2020-21.  This occurs 
when the annual growth on existing property is greater than the rate of inflation as measured by the 
Consumer Price Index.   Additional information will be received by June 1 regarding taxable values and 
the millage reduction factor.   
Oakland Schools has developed the Literacy Essentials Oakland (LEO) initiative in response to declining 
grade students using a collaborative program with all 28 constituent districts.  The program focuses on 
professional learning for teachers and administrators, a literacy coach in -residence program, 
personalized action plans for each participating district and a viable literacy curriculum with a balanced 
assessment system. The program will be evaluated by an external evaluator throughout the duration of 
the project. More detailed information regarding the goals of each  year of the program can be found in 
the Enterprise Wide Executive Summary.    
Career and Technical Education 
To help align ourselves with the Michigan Department of Education’s Top 10 in 10 Years goals and 
strategies, we are dedicating additional resourc es to career and technical education.  In addition to 
providing significant resources to our four technical campuses, we included additional funds in the  
2020-21 budget for career program redesign and initial funding for a CTE mobile classroom which is 
intended to bring STEM activities to local districts beginning in 2020 -21.  In 2019- 20, we increased the 
CTE allocation to LEA’s by more than 25% and are continuing that level of funding in 2020- 21.  This will 
provide local districts with additional resources for their local CTE programs from elementary to high 
school. 
Shared Services 
Oakland Schools provides various services to Oakland County districts that go above and beyond our 
normal service model.   These services include technology, business office, ins truction, and truancy 
services provided on a cost recovery basis.  Technology services are currently being provided to thirteen 
constituent districts and business office services are currently being provided on a full or part-time basis 
to five districts through an intergovernmental agreement (IGA) with Oakland Schools. 
MiPEER Consortium  
In FY 2012 -13, Oakland Schools and 22 Oak land County school districts formed the Michigan 
Partnership for Essential Education Resources (MIPEER) to select a countywide Enterprise Resource 
Planning system for human resource and financial applications.  Since then the Consortium has engaged 
in standardizing the Chart of Accounts and created many best practice business process for each

Illuminate  
Oakland Schools has contracted with Illuminate Education as our county -wide student assessment 
and data analysis tool vend or since the Fall of 2016 and all twenty -eight (28) districts and two (2) 
public school academies are included in the software license and support agreement. 
 
Illuminate DnA provides teachers with one place to seamlessly build and administer formative 
assessments, capture and analyze multiple sources of data to inform instruction, and directs 
student to learning resources needed to support specific, targeted standards. 
 
The Oakland Schools Board of Education has agreed to provide a 50% subsidy for the student 
assessment software and support again in 2020-21.  Additional add-ons, called “Item Banks,” can be 
purchased through the contract at the districts’ cost.  
 
Subsidies 
The Illuminate DnA subsidy referenced above is just one of several subsidies provided by Oakland 
Schools.  We provide multiple software products at no cost or a reduced cost to constituent districts 
in the areas of Career and Technical Education and curriculum and assessment.  Additionally, a 
subsidy is provided on the per pupil cost of the PowerSchool BusinessPlus system for districts who 
are members of the MiPEER consortium.  Communication tools such as news services and media 
monitoring/clipping services are provided to LEA’s at no cost.  These are just a few of the financial 
subsidies provided in the 2020-21 budget and are exclusive of pass-through grant funds, other 
district allocations and the cost of dedicated staff who support student and finance/HR software 
and applications.  
                                      
Personnel Costs 
In addition to step increases for those employees who are eligible, an across-the-board salary increase of 
1.0% has been built into the budget, subject to collective bargaining.   
 
Regarding the State-mandated retirement rate, there are 8 rates in effect, depending on the hire date of 
employees and their choices for eventual retirement benefits. The most common employee choice is that 
for Basic/MIP with Health Care Premium Subsidy, and that rate is projected to increase from 27.5% to 
28.21% beginning October 1, 2020.  Oakland Schools analyzed its own employee choices and has 
projected an overall blended retirement rate of 28.0% for 2020-21.  
 
The “Hard Cap” for employee health care costs remains in effect for all labor groups.   The hard cap dollar 
limits that employers may pay are subject to annual adjustment based on the medical price index, which 
the school district has no jurisdiction over.  Oakland Schools has projected growth in the medical price 
index of 3.0%.  If this projection proves to be accurate, the dollar limits that will be in effect will grow to:

Oakland Schools is dedicated to sound financial planning and preparing for the economic uncertainties 
in the future.  In that regard, we utilize a five-year forecast that is updated with each budget amendment 
and incorporates projections from Oakland County Equalization for taxable values.  We also maintain a 
five-year capital plan that is updated annually to prepare for necessary improvements and upgrades to 
facilities and equipment.  The organization pre-funds at least three years and up to five years of the 
capital plan requirements.   
Please be assured that we will continue to work hard to ensure that the budget reflects our continued 
commitment to increasing student achievement, using economies of scale to decrease operating costs 
for local districts, customizing and regionalizing programs and services to meet the diverse needs of our 
constituent school districts, and assisting schools and districts in meeting state and federal mandates. 
Per the Michigan School Code, Section 380.624(2), the proposed FY 2020-21 General Fund budget is to 
be submitted to its constituent district Board of Education for review by May 1.  Not later than June 1, 
the board of each constituent district shall adopt a resolution of support or non-support of the General 
Fund budget.  A presentation of the budget to our 28 local school district business managers is 
scheduled to occur at their meeting of the Oakland County School Business Officials on April 17, 
2020.  The Oakland Schools Board of Education will then hold a Designates Meeting on April 28 at 6:00 
PM in Conference Room A, providing local board designates and district administration an opportunity 
to review the budget prior to submitting their board’s resolution to Oakland Schools on or before June 1, 
2020. 
The Oakland Schools Board of Education will hold a Truth in Budgeting hearing on June 1, and will 
consider the FY 2020-21 proposed budget documents for approval during its regular meeting that night.

Human Sexuality Unit: 
Focus Questions: 
What changes, physically and emotionally can I expect in my future regarding puberty? 
What are the negative consequences of unhealthy sexual behaviors?

Grade Level Content Expectations 
5: PERSONAL HEALTH AND WELLNESS 
128
Standard 1: Core Concepts 
5.2 Explain how common infectious diseases are transmitted by air, indirect contact, and 
person-to-person contact 
Standard 2: Access Information 
5.3 Locate resources in one's school and community, and on the Internet, related to personal 
health issues and concerns; and assess the validity of the resources. 
Standard 3: Health Behaviors 
5.4 Apply health practices that can prevent the spread of illness, including foodborne illness. 
5.6 Analyze the characteristics of an illness or injury to determine whether it is necessary to seek 
medical care. 
6: HIV AND OTHER STIs PREVENTION 
Standard 1: Core Concepts 
6.1 Explain how HIV is and is not transmitted. 
6.2 Distinguish between facts and myths regarding HIV infection and AIDS. 
Standard 2: Access Information 
6.3 Explain when it is important to get adult, medical, and/or counseling help. 
6.4 Describe sources of accurate information and assistance in one's community. 
Standard 3: Health Behaviors 
6.5 Analyze behaviors and situations that may result in increased risk for HIV and other sexually 
transmitted infections (STIs). 
6.6 Analyze situations where assertive communication and refusal skills can be used to avoid 
and escape risky situations. 
7: GROWTH AND DEVELOPMENT 
Standard 1: Core Concepts 
7.1 Summarize the benefits of staying within behavioral limits and remaining abstinent. 
7.2 Compare characteristics of healthy and unhealthy relationships, and describe ways to 
express caring for a boyfriend or girlfriend while staying abstinent. 
Standard 3: Health Behaviors 
7.3 Set personal boundaries and limits related to physical intimacy and sexual behavior. 
7.4 Demonstrate skills to avoid and escape risky situations. 
Standard 4: Influences 
7.5 Examine viewpoints of parents and other trusted adults regarding teen relationships, 
abstinence, and sexual decisions. 
7.6 Evaluate the impact of alcohol and other drug use on decisions regarding sexual behavior.

9th Grade Health 
Unit 6:  Growth and Development 
Focus Questions 
What is sexual/emotional intimacy? 
What are ways to develop abstinence skills? 
Why is abstinence the best choice? 
What is the law of consent in Michigan? 
What are the functions of the male reproductive system? 
What are the ways to keep the male reproductive system healthy? 
What are the functions of the female reproductive system? 
What are the ways to keep the female reproductive system healthy? 
Why is using contraception a responsible decision for those who choose to be sexually active? 
What are the different types of contraception? 
Key Concepts and Skills 
• Risks of sexual intimacy 
• Abstinence refusal skills 
• Law of consent 
• Functions of male reproductive system 
• Functions of female reproductive system 
• Diagrams of female and male reproductive system 
• Pregnancy prevention 
• Unreliable methods of contraception 
• Permanent methods of contraception 
• Protection against Sexually transmitted diseases/infections 
High School Content Expectations 
PERSONAL HEALTH AND WELLNESS 
130
Standard 1: Core Concepts 
5.2 Explain the importance of regular health screenings or exams. 
7: GROWTH AND DEVELOPMENT 
Standard 1: Core Concepts 
7.1 Summarize and explain laws related to the sexual behavior of young people. 
7.2 Compare and contrast the pros and cons of methods used for pregnancy and disease 
prevention, including abstinence and use of contraception. 
7.3 Describe routine medical screening and examinations for maintaining reproductive health, 
and medical tests for pregnancy, HIV, and other STIs: who should be tested, the procedures 
used, and the importance of early detection and care. 
Standard 3: Health Behaviors 
7.5 Apply strategies, including refusal and assertiveness skills to avoid, manage, and escape 
situations that are high risk for pregnancy, HIV, and other STIs. 
Standard 4: Influences 
7.7 Evaluate the physical, social, emotional, legal, and economic impacts of teen pregnancy, 
teen parenting, HIV infection, or other STIs on personal lifestyle, goal achievement, friends, and 
family members. 
Standard 5: Goal Setting 
7.8 Develop personal goals and a specific plan for using the best contraceptive or disease-
prevention method, including abstinence, for individual circumstances. 
Standard 6: Decision Making 
7.9 Apply decision-making skills to avoid situations that are high risk for pregnancy, HIV, and 
other STIs. 
Standard 7: Social Skills 
7.10 Demonstrate the ability to establish positive relationships, communicate caring and love 
without sexual intercourse, and communicate personal, sexual limits and values to a girlfriend or 
boyfriend.

Secondary Health Materials Adoption 
May 5, 2020 
Submitted by Natalie Haezebrouck 
Rationale 
This materials adoption is part of the Troy School District regular nine-year adoption cycle. The health 
education needs of students are constantly evolving based on the health-needs and concerns present in 
society.  It is important that we update and examine our curriculum regularly to ensure our students are 
getting the most current information, presented in an age appropriate way.  It has been many years 
since the last adoption of health materials, and the current textbooks are very outdated.  The health 
teacher team has been supplementing with free online resources along the way, but a common 
resource would help them ensure a guaranteed and viable curriculum for students. 
Process of Textbook Selection 
In September of 2019, the entire Middle and High School Health team met to collaboratively identify 
the potential publishers, materials, and resources to be considered for adoption.  Also, included were an 
assistant principal and the director of teaching and learning.

The health team has been working the past several years to align their curriculum.  They identified 
common units-of-study and a less/more chart (see appendix) to guide their work.  These pieces, along 
with State and National Health Education Standards, were the foundation for choosing appropriate 
materials for middle and high school health.

All health teachers spent several months evaluating materials.  Table 3 summarizes the texts that were 
reviewed by the team. In fall of 2019, the resource list was narrowed down, and the publisher from G-W 
was invited to Troy to demonstrate some of the online features that accompany the text resources.  
Teachers then tried out various components of the program with students, including the online platform 
with Schoology integration.  Throughout this process, the team studied current thinking in health 
education and also consulted with Christina Harvey, Oakland Schools Health Education Consultant, to gain 
her perspective on best practice in health education.   
Table 3: Textbooks Reviewed  
• Brynes Health Education Center Programs 
• Goodheart-Wilcox (G-W):  Comprehensive Health 
• Michigan Model for Health Education 
• Pearson:  Health 
After much dialogue, both seventh and ninth grade health teams decided to recommend the Goodheart-
Wilcox:  Comprehensive Health resource.  After making this determination, the Sex Education Advisory Board 
(SEAB) was gathered on April 30, 2020 to review the recommended materials.  During this dialogue, the SEAB 
praised the work of the health team.  Several Troy students participated on this committee and were able to 
share valuable insights about why a resource like this is important for students.  The SEAB unanimously voted 
in favor of the recommended resource.  The team also encouraged the district to pursue demonstration of 
contraception in high-school health, as well as sharing of public health resources that students can access 
digitally.  The minutes for the SEAB meeting are in the appendix.

Sexual Education Advisory Board Minutes 
Meeting Minutes  – April 30, 2020 at 6:30 PM 
In attendance:  Cherie Armstrong, Katie Austin, Lisa Danhoff, Christine DiPilato, Natalie Haezebrouck, Kristy Pierce 
Hall, Christina Harvey, Nicole Jenkins, Kelly Jones, Peg Kennedy, Andrea Lothian, Randy Maddock, Ann O’Neill, David 
Roth, Harleen Singh, Noah Scudder, and Patti Stark. 
Kristy Hall facilitated the meeting and began with introductions.  She explained the goal of this meeting of the 
Sexual Education Advisory Board (SEAB) is to review and discuss the health materials recommended by the health 
teachers for use in the middle school and high school health classes.  The charge of the SEAB is to ensure the 
materials are in alignment with the values and policies of the Troy School District and the community, and includes 
appropriate content as required by the Michigan Department of Education. 
Christina Harvey, health curriculum consultant from Oakland Schools, explained health curriculum includes topics 
about puberty, adolescence, healthy relationships, positive communication skills, identifying risky behaviors, legal 
consequences of risky behaviors, and Michigan law regarding required content.  Michigan law requires instruction in 
communicable diseases, such as STDs, HIV/AIDS, and sex education that focuses on abstinence.  Additional required 
content includes instruction about age appropriate and medically accurate information about sex, healthy 
relationships, how to identify signs of abuse, refusal skills, how to resist pressure to engage in risky behavior, 
adoption services, and the law regarding sexual contact.  Christina also noted that school districts may not distribute 
contraception, may not teach about abortion as part of family planning, or refer students to abortion clinics on 
school grounds.  
Natalie Haezebrouck, director of the Department of Teaching and Learning in the Troy School District, explained the 
collaborative process by which the 7th grade and 9th grade health teachers reviewed materials from multiple 
publishers over the last several months.  The textbooks published by The Goodheart-Wilcox Company (G-W) were 
recommended by the textbook selection committee.  The materials have the most current content with what 
students experience, and the publisher provides continuous updates to the online content. 
7  
140
Natalie also explained that parents can choose to opt out of having their student participate in specific chapters, 
units, or lessons involving sexual education topics.  Lisa Danhoff and Andrea Lothian explained that, in their 
experience, very few students opt out.  At Troy High, Lisa has only had eight students opt out over 18 years, and 
Athens has had similar numbers.  At Larson Middle School, Andrea has only had two or three students opt out 
during her teaching career. 
Andrea Lothian, health teacher at Larson Middle School, introduced the recommended G-W publication for middle 
school health classes, Comprehensive Health Skills for Middle School, 2nd Edition, copyright 2021, written by 
Catherine A. Sanderson, PhD, professor of psychology at Amherst College, and Mark Zelman, PhD, professor of 
biology at Aurora University.  Andrea explained that this publication feels new and up-to-date.  The main 
differences from the old textbook are in Unit 8 where sexuality, sexual orientation, and gender identity are 
discussed.  Andrea explained that they want to teach to all students and not to only a specific group.  Chapter 20 
covers responsible decisions and pregnancy prevention.  Students are taught abstinence, and the teachers always 
emphasize that abstinence is the best option.  However, the teachers believe it is essential to have the information 
available to the students.  The review committee had intense discussion regarding technology and the impact it has 
on where students acquire information.  Medically accurate information is important, and the teachers need to be 
able to provide healthy, accurate information to our students. 
Lisa Danhoff, health teacher at Troy High School, introduced the recommended G-W publication for high school 
health classes, Comprehensive Health Skills, 3rd Edition, copyright 2021, written by Catherine A. Sanderson, PhD, 
professor of psychology at Amherst College, and Mark Zelman, PhD, professor of biology at Aurora University.  Lisa 
explained the primary differences from the old textbook to the proposed textbook are in the sections covering teen 
pregnancy and parenthood (chapter 21).  The G-W information is explained in much more detail than other 
materials reviewed.  The chart listing methods of contraception is more comprehensive.  Emergency contraception 
is new in this book.  The subject is asked frequently in class.  Legal issues regarding fatherhood and custody options 
are included in this chapter.  Adoption is discussed.  Abortion is covered in the textbook, but it is not discussed in 
class.  The recommended textbook is factual and not trying to persuade the reader.  Chapter 23 of the G-W 
textbook has the most comprehensive and current information regarding understanding sexuality as compared to 
other materials reviewed.  Chapter 24 covers prevention of pregnancy.  Methods of contraception are explained in 
more detail.  Some terminology has changed regarding condoms.  For example, internal/external vs. female/male 
condoms.  Lisa created a detailed chart of updated information.  
8  
141
Member comments:   
• Kelly Jones commented that she would like to see the district and schools take a leadership role to form a group or 
safe place for LGBTQI students to meet.   
• Noah Scudder expressed the topic of gender identity is very important to cover in class.  Middle school is brutal for 
students, and their social and emotional well-being is very important.   
• Randy Maddock expressed concern that the course is only 9-10 weeks in length, and there is a lot of content to 
cover in a short period of time.   
• Cheri Armstrong appreciated that a section on vaping was included in the textbooks.   
• Ann O’Neill expressed her thoughts that this book is much better than the old book on inclusion, sexuality, and birth 
control.  She expressed that it could be a good reference for students as opposed to “Google.”   
• Kelly Jones asked if there are other resources provided regarding sexual health.   
• Katie Austin suggested a list of approved online resources be available for teachers to provide to students when 
requested.    
• David Roth asked how much is discussed about HIV in class.  Lisa said the basics are discussed in high school, but not 
as in-depth.   
There was some dialogue about demonstration of condom use in the classroom.  Discussion continued about how 
to help students develop skills to discuss condom use with their partner and how to negotiate with their partner if 
the other person is resistant to using a condom. 
The SEAB approved the teachers to research ways to demonstrate condom use in the classroom and the creation of 
a list of online resources regarding sexuality.  The materials review committee has been charged with vetting the 
options to be brought back to the SEAB for approval. 
A roll call vote was taken, and the new Goodheart-Wilcox textbooks mentioned above were unanimously approved 
for use by the Troy School District upon school board approval. 
Meeting adjourned at 7:35 PM.

Standard 1: Core Concepts 
5.2 Explain how common infectious diseases are transmitted by air, indirect contact, and person-to-person 
contact 
Standard 2: Access Information 
5.3 Locate resources in one's school and community, and on the Internet, related to personal health issues 
and concerns; and assess the validity of the resources. 
Standard 3: Health Behaviors 
5.4 Apply health practices that can prevent the spread of illness, including foodborne illness. 
5.6 Analyze the characteristics of an illness or injury to determine whether it is necessary to seek medical 
care. 
6: HIV AND OTHER STIs PREVENTION 
Standard 1: Core Concepts 
6.1 Explain how HIV is and is not transmitted. 
6.2 Distinguish between facts and myths regarding HIV infection and AIDS. 
Standard 2: Access Information 
6.3 Explain when it is important to get adult, medical, and/or counseling help. 
6.4 Describe sources of accurate information and assistance in one's community. 
Standard 3: Health Behaviors 
6.5 Analyze behaviors and situations that may result in increased risk for HIV and other sexually transmitted 
infections (STIs). 
6.6 Analyze situations where assertive communication and refusal skills can be used to avoid and escape 
risky situations. 
7: GROWTH AND DEVELOPMENT 
Standard 1: Core Concepts 
7.1 Summarize the benefits of staying within behavioral limits and remaining abstinent. 
7.2 Compare characteristics of healthy and unhealthy relationships, and describe ways to express caring for 
a boyfriend or girlfriend while staying abstinent. 
Standard 3: Health Behaviors 
7.3 Set personal boundaries and limits related to physical intimacy and sexual behavior. 
7.4 Demonstrate skills to avoid and escape risky situations. 
Standard 4: Influences 
7.5 Examine viewpoints of parents and other trusted adults regarding teen relationships, abstinence, and 
sexual decisions. 
7.6 Evaluate the impact of alcohol and other drug use on decisions regarding sexual behavior.

 TO:       Rick West - Asst. Superintendent - Business Services

 FROM:  Rob Carson – Director of Maintenance & Operations   
Department 
RE:       BP 29A – Niles Relocation Project –  
1140 Rankin 
Ph: 248.823.4050 
Fax: 248.823.4077 
DATE:   April 29, 2020 
RFP (9908) was issued to obtain competitive bids for renovation work for the Niles 
Relocation Project.  This work was part of the original bid package; however, we did not 
receive any bids for this category, so it was not awarded.  The scope of work for this 
category of the Niles Relocation Project is exterior aluminum storefront/windows and 
exterior doors & door hardware as specified in the bid documents.     
During Barton Malow’s post bid interviews it was identified that the apparent low bidder 
(Modern Mirror & Glass Co.) did not include the electrified hardware which was specified and 
part of the bid specifications.  The contractor acknowledged that they could not perform the 
work for their bid price due to the scope they missed.  The post bid interview with Advanced 
Building Group did confirm complete coverage of work scope and verified technical skills, 
therefore, I am recommending the Exterior Doors and Aluminum be awarded to Advance 
Building Group as the low qualified bidder.  A Bid Summary and Contract Award 
Recommendation from the Barton Malow Company is attached.     
Category                    Contractor     Amount 
080000 – Exterior Doors & Aluminum  Advanced Building Group   $111,882.00  
A separate Purchase Order will be issued to ABG for this Bid Category.  The funding for th is 
purchase will be from the Niles Relocation Project Fund. 
If you have any questions, please let me know.

Re: Troy School District - Bid Package 29:  Niles Relocation Building 
BP 29A - Contract Award Recommendation – Aluminum Storefront
Dear Mr. West:
Proposals were received on April 14, 2020 for Bid Package 29A- Niles relocation – Aluminum Exterior 
Doors.  Previously, when the original Bid Package 29 – Niles Relocation Project was bid we received no 
bidder turnout for the Aluminum Storefront / Glazing work due to the small amount of work in this 
package.  After conversations with the administration team, we re-bid the project as it is required for the 
completion of the Nile Relocation project.  With that being said, the recommendation information is listed 
below.  A bid tabulation summary of the bids received is attached.  
We received two bid proposals for this project.  Of those bids the low bidder at bid opening did not 
include all of the required scope / specified work which was to include the electrified hardware listed in 
the bidding documents.  This became obvious upon the post bid review with the Contractor when their 
bid was reviewed and they noted they did not include those specified items.  With that being the case, we 
reviewed the second bidder on the project and qualifications of apparent second bidder, and recommend 
contract be awarded as follows:
Category Contractor   Amount
080000 – Exterior Doors and Alum Advanced Building Group $     111,882
TOTAL AWARD $     111,882
Included with this award recommendation is a tabulation spreadsheet of bids received on April 14, 2020 
for BP 29A. 
Please contact me should you have any questions.

A.  CONSIDERATION:   Biennial Election for Oakland Schools Board of 
Education on June 1, 2020 
In accordance with Public Act 419 of 2004, the Troy Board of Education is 
required to elect board of education members to the Oakland Schools 
Board of Education and select a designate to represent the Troy Board of 
Education to cast a ballot on its behalf .  At this evening’s meeting, the 
Board will designate a representative to cast a ballot at the Oakland 
Schools Board of Education meeting on June 1st and will then consider 
adopting a resolution as prescribed by the revised school code.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION :  Discussion of candidate, selection of TSD Board 
Representative and acceptance of the resolution below.   
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
RESOLUTION OF THE TROY BOARD OF EDUCATION 
WHEREAS, Public Act 419 of 2004 amended the election procedures for 
Intermediate School Districts that elect board members at a meeting of 
representatives from constituent district school boards, and 
WHEREAS, Public Act 419 of 2004 requires a constituent school board to designate 
its representative and identify the Intermediate School Board candidate the board 
supports by a resolution ado pted not earlier than 21 days prior to the date of the 
election, and 
WHEREAS, Public Act 419 of 2004 prescribes the method for passage of a 
resolution including the requirement to consider the resolution at not less than one 
public meeting before adopting the resolution, and 
WHEREAS, the Board previously considered the resolution at an open meeting 
conducted in a manner prescribed under the Open Meetings Act on May 19, 2020.

Oakland Schools Board of Education  
2020 Election Timeline 
January 24, 2020:   
Candidates may pick up nominating petitions and affidavit forms at the Oakland County 
Clerk’s Office, Elections Division [1200 N. Telegraph Road, Dept. 417, Pontiac, MI 
48341]. 
May 1, 2020 - 4:00 PM:  
Last day for candidates to file nominating petitions, filing fees, and affidavits of identity 
with the Oakland County Clerk’s Office, Elections Division. 
May 4, 2020: 
Oakland Schools receives list of qualified candidates from Oakland County Clerk’s 
Office, Elections Division. Oakland Schools sends list of qualified candidates to 
constituent district superintendents and executive assistants to the superintendents. 
May 11, 2020:  
Each constituent district board of education must: 
1) Designate its representative and identify the ISD candidate(s) its board supports. 
2) Consider and adopt a resolution affirming #1. Each board must consider its 
resolution at not less than one (1) public meeting before adopting the resolution.   
May 29, 2020 – 4:00 PM:  
Copies of district resolutions due to the Oakland Schools administrative office via fax 
(248.209.2206) or email (Nicole.Christian@oakland.k12.mi.us). 
June 1, 2020 - 5:30 – 7:30 PM: 
District representatives attend Oakland Schools Board of Education Biennial Board 
Election open meeting to cast ballots on behalf of their districts. No absentee ballots can 
be accepted. 
June 2, 2020: 
Notification of election results sent from Oakland Schools to constituent district 
superintendents, executive assistants to the superintendents, and Oakland County Clerk’s 
Office, Elections Division. 
July 7, 2020: 
Successful candidates take oath of office at Oakland Schools Board of Education 
Organizational Meeting, if not present at the Biennial Board Election.

March 25, 2020 
Dear Local District Board Secretary: 
The purpose of this letter is to advise you that an election for one (1) six-year term on the Oakland 
Schools Board of Education will be held on Monday, June 1, 2020. 
Oakland Schools board members are elected by an electoral body composed of one (1) person designated 
by the board of each constituent school district. The Revised School Code prescribes that each constituent 
school district board must designate a representative to this electoral body by adopting a resolution which:   
a. Designates the person to vote in the election on behalf of the local district board; 
b. Directs the designee which candidate the board supports for each position to be filled; 
and 
c. Directs the des ignee to vote for those individuals, at least on the first ballot taken by 
the electoral body.  
Designated electors must cast their ballots in person at the election scheduled to take place between 5:30 
– 7:30 PM on Monday, June 1, 2020 in the Oakland Schools Conference Center located at 2111 Pontiac 
Lake Road in Waterford. The revised ISD election law prohibits the acceptance of absentee ballots. 
After your board has adopted its resolution as prescribed, please provide a copy of the resolution to the 
Oakland Schools Superintendent’s office by Friday, May 29, 2020 via facsimile 248.209.2206 or email 
Nicole.Christian@oakland.k12.mi.us. This will enable us to compile a list of district designees prior to 
the election date. 
Enclosed is a copy of the 2020 Election Timeline, Revised School Code, the Agenda for the June 1 
Election Meeting, Candidate Nominating Guidelines, and a sample resolution to designate your district’s 
representative and selection of candidate(s).  
If you have any questions regarding the ISD legislation regarding board elections or the ISD board 
election process, please contact Sherry McMillan at 248.209.2062 or by email at 
Sherry.McMillan@oakland.k12.mi.us.  
Your assistance is greatly appreciated. 
Sincerely, 
Barb DeMarco 
Barb DeMarco, Secretary  
Oakland Schools Board of Education

Good afternoon Superintendents and Executive Assistants, 
The following individual has filed as a candidate for the six‐year term seat open for the 2020 Oakland 
Schools Board of Education Biennial Election. 
 Mr. George Ehlert 
Oakland Schools board members are elected by an electoral body composed of one (1) person 
designated by the board of each constituent school district. The Revised School Code prescribes that 
each constituent school district board must designate a representative to this electoral body by 
adopting a resolution that will: 
1. Designate a person to vote in the election on behalf of the local district board. 
2. Direct the designee as to which candidate the local district board supports to fill the open seat.  
3. Direct the designee to vote for the individual, at least on the first ballot taken by the electoral 
body.  
The designated electors must cast their ballots at the election scheduled to take place between 5:30 – 
7:30 PM on Monday, June 1, 2020 in Conference Room B of the Oakland Schools main building 
located at 2111 Pontiac Lake Road in Waterford. The revised school code prohibits the acceptance of 
absentee ballots.  Please note, you will be notified if an alternative voting location is deemed 
necessary due to the Governor’s Executive Order. 
According to the revised school code, districts shall consider the resolution at no less than one (1) 
meeting before adopting the resolution. Districts have interpreted this language differently and 
historically have used one of the following three options to consider and adopt their resolutions: 
 Consider the resolution as a discussion item during an open meeting and adopt the resolution 
as an action item during another open meeting, with each meeting occurring on a separate 
date 
 Consider the resolution as a discussion item during an open meeting and adopt the resolution 
as an action item during a second open meeting immediately following the first open meeting 
1
158
 Consider the resolution as a discussion item and adopt the resolution as an action item at the 
same open meeting (Local school districts selecting this option should seek independent legal 
counsel confirming a singular open meeting to consider and adopt a resolution that is 
compliant with school code. 
According to the revised school code, districts must adopt their resolutions no earlier than twenty‐
one (21) days prior to the election. After your board has adopted its resolution as prescribed by the 
revised school code, please submit  a copy of the resolution to the Oakland Schools’ Superintendent’s 
office via email to nicole.christian@oakland.k12.mi.us by the end of the business day on Friday, May 
29, 2020. This will enable us to compile a list of district designees prior to the election date. Attached 
is a copy of the Revised School Code, a sample resolution template to designate your district’s 
representative and selection of candidates, and an election timeline. An official notice of this election 
will be sent to your board of education secretary via registered mail. 
If you have any questions regarding board elections or the ISD board election process please contact 
Sherry McMillan at 248.209.2062   or by email at Sherry.McMillan@oakland.k12.mi.us. 
Note: As the candidate is the incumbent and running un‐opposed, a “Meet the Candidates” forum will 
not be scheduled. Thank you. 
Nicole Christian 
Executive Assistant to  the Superintendent & Board of Education  
2111 Pontiac Lake Rd., Waterford, MI 48328

THE REVISED SCHOOL CODE (EXCERPT)
Act 451 of 1976
and affidavit; ballots; filing fee.
in June by an electoral body composed of 1 person designated by the board of each constituent school district.
(2) The board of a constituent district shall designate its representative to this electoral body by resolution
adopted not earlier than 21 days before the date of this biennial election. The board shall consider the
resolution at not less than 1 public meeting before adopting the resolution. The resolution shall be adopted by
majority vote of the members serving on the board. In its resolution designating its representative, the board
of a constituent district shall identify the candidate the board supports for each position to be filled on the
intermediate school board and shall direct its representative to vote for that individual or individuals at least
on the first ballot taken by the electoral body. The secretary of the intermediate school board shall send a
notice by certified mail of the hour and place of the meeting of the electoral body described in subsection (1)
to the secretary of the board of each constituent school district at least 10 days before the meeting. The
president and secretary of the intermediate school board shall act as chairperson and secretary at the meeting.
The meeting of the electoral body shall be an open meeting conducted in the manner prescribed under the
open meetings act, 1976 PA 267, MCL 15.261 to 15.275.
(3) Except as provided in section 703, the term of office of each member elected to the intermediate school
board is 6 years and begins on July 1 following election. Not more than 2 members of the intermediate school
board shall be from the same school district unless there are fewer districts than there are positions to be
filled.
(4) A vacancy shall be filled by the remaining members of the intermediate school board until the next
biennial election at which time the vacancy shall be filled for the balance of the unexpired term. Notice of the
vacancy shall be filed with the state board within 5 days after the vacancy occurs. If the vacancy is not filled
within 30 days after it occurs, the vacancy shall be filled by the state board.
(5) Subject to subsection (7), a candidate for election to the intermediate school board shall be nominated
by petitions that are signed by a number of school electors of the combined constituent school districts of the
intermediate school district, as follows:
(a) If the population of the intermediate school district is less than 10,000 according to the most recent
federal census, a minimum of 6 and a maximum of 20.
(b) If the population of the intermediate school district is 10,000 or more according to the most recent
federal census, a minimum of 40 and a maximum of 100.
(6) A school elector may sign as many petitions as there are vacancies to fill. Nominating petitions and an
affidavit as provided in section 558 of the Michigan election law, MCL 168.558, shall be filed with the school
district filing official not later than 30 days before the date of the biennial election under subsection (1). The
school district filing official shall determine the sufficiency of the petitions and the eligibility of the
candidates nominated. The school district filing official shall provide ballots for the biennial election, listing
on the ballots the names of all candidates properly nominated. The chairperson of the biennial election
meeting may accept nominations for a vacancy from the floor only if no nominating petitions have been filed
for the vacancy.
(7) Instead of filing nominating petitions, a candidate for election to the intermediate school board may pay
a nonrefundable filing fee of $100.00 to the school district filing official. If this fee is paid by the due date for
nominating petitions, the payment has the same effect under this section as the filing of nominating petitions.

resolution des ignating their district ’s Superintendent (or his/her designee) as that 
district’s proposed representative as an a t-large member of the Governing Body are 
Rochester Com munity Schools  (Superintendent Dr. Robert Shaner)  and Birmingham 
Public Schools (Superintendent Mark Dziatczak); and 
 WHEREAS, the Board has been presented with a ballot created by the Fiscal 
Agent representatives with the names of Rochester Community Sch ools Superintendent 
Dr. Robert Shaner (or his  designee) and Birmingham Public Schools Sup erintendent 
Mark Dziatczak (or his designee) for vote to serve as the two at -large members of the 
Governing Body for two-year terms; and 
 WHEREAS, the Board deems it to be in the best interests of the District to cast 
its vote for the above-mentioned Superintendents (or their designees) to serve as the 
two at-large members of the Governing Body;  
 NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 1. The Board hereby casts its vote to have Rochester Community 
  Schools Superintendent Dr. Robert Shaner (or his designee) and  
  Birmingham Public Schools Superintendent Mark Dziatczak (or his  
  designee) serve as the two at-large members of the IA Governing 
  Body.  
2.       All other resolutions and parts of resolutions insofar as they conflict 
  with the provisions of this resolution are hereby rescinded. 
Roll call vote.
